# Animation registry — every formula, dial and kinetics symbol, with its status

Generated by `python content/video_engine/scripts/build_animation_registry.py --write`. Never
hand-edited: the recipe below is the whole of it, and `--check` fails when this file drifts from it.

**220 code · 150 dial · 349 formula records — 452 implemented, 67 tracked, 4 retired, 46 orphaned.**
**Provenance of the formula records — 266 sourced, 53 derived, 30 unsourced.**

**Read** — code: `content/video_engine/scripts/kinetics/*.mjs` (every `export const|function`, plus
every key of an exported constant object). Dials: those same constant objects, and the player
template's `MARK` / `SP` / `LP` / `MOUNT_STEPS` / `DISSOLVE_S` / `LP_MOUNT_RISE` / `CAP_LAST_HOLD_S`.
Formulas: `docs/content-video-engine/{29,42..53}-*.md`, `FINDING-the-animation-math-*.md`,
`briefs/ANSWERS-RESEARCH-BRIEF-animation-craft.md`, and the `reference_analyses` files named
ANIMATION / DRAWING / KINETIC / academic_literature — every `$$` block, every `=` line carrying a
mathematical symbol, every line naming a law. Chain roots also read the headers, comments and rule
constants of `content/video_engine/scripts/gate_*.py`.

**Status** — precedence implemented > retired > tracked > orphaned.
*implemented*: a kinetics module cites the formula's section, or the name is written in a module or
the template (code: the player calls it, a test names it, or another kinetics line uses it), or the
citation chain below reaches it. *retired*: a BACKLOG or doc-47 row naming it says reclassified /
withdrawn / closed / invented **in its status text** (`closed-form` is not `closed`, and the word
inside a bolded title is a title, not a verdict). *tracked*: such a row names it, or cites its
section in `docs/DOCS-CITATIONS.jsonl`, or graduates it (`**CLOSED <date> by <doc>**` is a
graduation to a written standard, not a withdrawal — the target doc is the evidence).
*orphaned*: none of those — stated and unattached.

**The citation chain (depth 2)** — the kinetics modules cite our numbered doc sections (`42 s42.1`),
and those sections carry the research section the doc was extracted from. A research section reached
from an implemented code record — or from a gate's rule constant — through `code → our section →
research section` inherits *implemented*, with the chain as its evidence
(`…/stroke.mjs:5 → 42§42.1 → 07§1.2`). The second hop is read two ways: the ``07`` §5.3-style
references written in our section's body (a whole-doc cite reads the whole doc's body, so doc 45's
`06` §2–4 counts), and the disposition rows of `docs/content-video-engine/RESEARCH-INDEX.md`
(`| 1.2 Kinematic Arc-Length Reparameterization | EXTRACTED -> 42 SS42.1 |`). A reached section
covers its subsections. Without this a shipped law reads as an orphan on the research side, because
the module cites our doc and never the file the doc was extracted from.

**Provenance** (formula records) — *derived*: the section carries a `[DERIVED:` tag, or a BACKLOG /
doc-47 row naming it calls it invented, reclassified or derived. *sourced*: the record is itself in
`content/video_engine/sources/reference_analyses/`, or its section reaches such a file through the
chain above, or the section carries a `URL:`. *unsourced*: neither — a figure with nothing behind it.

**Named heuristics** — the template's `KINETICS:BEGIN..END` regions are `sync_kinetics.py`'s copies
of the modules, so uses inside them are not template use sites; a law is matched by its aliases and
anything else by the hyphen/slash compounds of its name (`Secondary-Motion`, `zero-slip`), never by
a common word; `zero-slip` and `FK/IK` were added to the law list because doc 48 states them as
rules; a kinetics constant key appears twice on purpose — as code surface (flag, sites, tests) and
as a dial (with its value); the research files are held twice on disk (a bundle copy and a top-level
copy), and a chain that reaches one reaches its byte-identical twin.

## Formulas and laws

### implemented (234)

- **1. Every stroke we draw is wrong in one specific, fixable way** — `κ^(−1/3)` sends velocity to infinity.
